But the new sepulcher can also signify the virginal womb of Mary; and the stone set against the door, and a great stone at that, can show that the sepulcher could not have been opened without the help of many.

And Mary Magdalene was there, and the other Mary, sitting opposite the sepulcher. While the others left the Lord, the women persevere in their duty, waiting for what Jesus had promised; and therefore they deserved to be the first to see him rising, because he who perseveres to the end, he shall be saved.

Command therefore that the sepulcher be guarded until the third day, lest perhaps his disciples come and steal him. It was not enough for the chief priests and the scribes and Pharisees to have crucified the Lord Savior, unless they also guarded the sepulcher, received a cohort, sealed the stone and, as far as lay in them, set their hand against the one rising, so that their diligence might profit our faith; for the more closely it is guarded, the more the power of the resurrection is shown. Hence also he was laid in a new tomb which had been hewn in the rock, lest, if it had been built of many stones, he should be said to have been taken away by theft after the foundations of the grave had been dug under. And that he was to be laid in a sepulcher, there is the testimony of the prophet who says: He dwelt in a lofty cave of the strongest rock, and immediately, after two little verses, there follows: You will see the King with glory.

Matthew 28

Now in the evening of the Sabbath, as it grows light toward the first day of the week, there came Mary Magdalene and the other Mary to see the sepulchre. That different times are described for these women in the Gospels is not a sign of falsehood, as the impious object, but the service of a diligent visiting, while they often go away and run back and do not bear to be away from the Lord's sepulchre long or far.

And behold, there was a great earthquake; for an angel of the Lord came down from heaven, and coming up he rolled back the stone and was sitting upon it; and his appearance was like lightning, and his clothing like snow. Our Lord, one and the same Son of God and Son of Man, according to each nature, of divinity and of flesh, shows now the signs of his greatness, now those of his lowliness. Hence in the present passage too, although it is a man who was crucified, who was buried, who was shut in the tomb, whom the stone set against it holds in, yet the things done outside show the Son of God: the sun fleeing, darkness rushing in, the earth shaken, the veil torn, the rocks split apart, the dead raised, the ministries of angels, which from the very beginning of his birth also proved him God. Gabriel comes to Mary, an angel speaks with Joseph, the same announces it to the shepherds, afterward a choir of angels is heard: Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace among men of good will, he is tempted in the wilderness, and after the victory angels at once serve him. Now too an angel comes as guardian of the Lord's sepulchre and in white clothing signifies the glory of the one who triumphs; and moreover, as the Lord ascends to heaven, two angels are seen on the Mount of Olives, promising the apostles the second coming of the Savior.

But for fear of him the guards were terrified and became like dead men; and the angel answered and said to the women: You, do not be afraid; for I know that you are seeking Jesus who was crucified. The guards, terrified with fear, lie stunned like dead men, and yet the angel consoles not them but the women. You, do not be afraid. Let them, he says, be afraid; let dread persist in those in whom unbelief remains; but you, because you are seeking Jesus crucified, hear that he has risen and has fulfilled what he promised.

Come and see where he had been laid, so that, if you do not believe my words, you may believe the empty sepulchre; and with hurried step go and announce to his disciples that he has risen and is going before you into Galilee, that is, into the wallowing-place of the Gentiles, where before there was error and slipperiness, and no footstep was planted with firm and steady foot.

And they went out quickly from the tomb with fear and great joy, running to announce it to his disciples. A twofold feeling, of fear and of joy, held the minds of the women: the one from the greatness of the miracle, the other from longing for the one rising again; and yet both spurred on the women's step. They were going to the apostles so that through them the seed of faith might be scattered.

And behold, Jesus met them, saying: Hail. Those who were seeking in this way, who were running so, deserved to have the risen Lord come to meet them and to be the first to hear: Hail, so that the curse of the woman Eve might be undone in women.

But they came up and took hold of his feet and worshiped him. These women come up and hold his feet because they worshiped him. But that woman who was seeking the living among the dead and did not yet know that the Son of God had risen rightly hears: Do not touch me, for I have not yet ascended to my Father.

Then Jesus said to them: Do not be afraid. In both the Old and the New Testament this must always be observed: that whenever some more august vision has appeared, fear is first driven out, so that, the mind being thus calmed, the things that are said may be heard.

Go, tell my brothers to go into Galilee; there they will see me. To those brothers of whom he also said in another place: I will announce your name to my brothers, who see the Savior not at all in Judea but in the multitude of the Gentiles.

And having gathered with the elders and taken counsel, they gave a large sum of money to the soldiers, saying: Say that his disciples came by night and stole him while we were asleep; and if this comes to the governor's hearing, we will persuade him and make you safe. The guards confess the miracle; they hurry back to the city; they report to the chief priests what they had seen, what they had beheld being done. Those who ought to have been turned to repentance and to seek the risen Jesus persist in malice, and they turn the money that had been given for the uses of the temple to the buying of a lie, just as before they had given thirty pieces of silver to Judas the traitor. All, therefore, who misuse the offerings of the temple and the things contributed for the uses of the Church on other things with which to satisfy their own will are like the scribes and priests who bought the lie and the blood of the Savior.

But the eleven disciples went into Galilee, to the mountain where Jesus had appointed for them. After the resurrection Jesus is seen on a mountain of Galilee and is worshiped there, although some doubt, and their doubt increases our faith. Then he is shown more plainly to Thomas, and he displays both his side wounded by the lance and his hands pierced with the nails.

Jesus came up and spoke to them, saying: All power has been given to me in heaven and on earth. Power has been given to him who a little before had been crucified, who had been buried in the tomb, who had lain dead, who afterward rose again. But power has been given in heaven and on earth so that he who before reigned in heaven may reign on earth through the faith of believers.

Go therefore, teach all nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit. First they teach all nations, then they dip those who have been taught in water. For it cannot be that the body receives the sacrament of baptism unless the soul has first received the truth of the faith. And they are baptized in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit so that, as their divinity is one, the bestowal may be one; and the name of the Trinity is one God.

Teaching them to observe all things whatever I have commanded you. A notable order. He commanded the apostles first to teach all the nations, then to dip them in the sacrament of faith, and after faith and baptism to enjoin what was to be observed. And lest we think that what was commanded is light and little, he added: all things whatever I have commanded you, so that those who have believed, who have been baptized in the Trinity, may do all things that have been commanded.

And behold, I am with you all days, even to the consummation of the age. He promises that he will be with the disciples even to the consummation of the age, and shows both that they will always live and that he will never withdraw from those who believe. But he who promises his presence even to the consummation of the world is not ignorant of that day on which he knows he will be with the apostles.
